This implies that Andrews poured the juice after discovering the body, which would mean the glass of juice was not there initially.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction. 
None of the provided spatial reasoning rules directly apply to identifying the contradiction between the crime scene photo (Evidence 9) and Andrews' testimony about pouring a glass of juice (Testimony 4). The contradiction arises from the implication that the glass of juice was already present in the crime scene photo, contradicting Andrews' claim of pouring herself a glass after finding the body.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction. 
The spatial reasoning here involves understanding the layout and state of the crime scene as described by the evidence (the crime photo) and comparing it with the actions described by Andrews in her testimony. The presence of the glass of juice in the crime photo and Andrews' statement about pouring a glass of juice after discovering the body create a temporal inconsistency regarding the state of the crime scene.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format.
